TEAM · Thesis Check · 2026.10.20

Seats 从"下滑"重置为"增长"，证据站得住吗

用 Atlassian 18 场管理层发言和 Knowledge Base 里的专家、卖方材料，逐条核对 Caffrey 的 seats 增长 thesis。原文在前，分析在后。

54份材料
18场管理层发言
462条逐字原文
0个 seat 数字

总体判断

口径：用 Atlassian 自己的 17 场业绩会和卖方会议 (2025.05–2026.09)、1 份 FactSet 会议转录，以及 Knowledge Base 里的专家访谈和卖方摘要，逐条核对"TEAM 的 seats 从预期下滑重置为持续增长"这个 thesis。每条引文都已用脚本对原文逐字核对，并补成完整原文段落 (不删节)，带文件名和行号。

Executive Summary

Seats 确实还在涨，这一点管理层每个季度都说，渠道商和前员工也没看到减少；但公司从来没给过 seat 数或增速，CFO 的原话是 seat 扩张速率"稳定了 4-6 个季度"，是稳定，不是加速。

过去一年的加速主要来自三件可以拆开的事：上云迁移 (每年给云收入贡献中到高个位数的增长)、Teamwork Collection 打包和版本升级、每年 7-10% 的提价。"Coding agent 让更多人进来、所以 seats 加速"这条链，唯一的数字是"用 AI 编码工具的客户 seat 扩张快 5%"，CEO 自己承认这批是增长型的前沿公司。

AI 带来的工作量增长远快于 seat：经 MCP 建的 Jira 工作项是 4 倍，MCP 调用一个季度涨 400%，而人通过网页和手机用产品"几乎没变"。这部分价值要靠 credits 和用量去收，12 月 3 日才开始收，还没进数字。

FY27 subscription ARR 指引 18%，隐含有机净新增 ARR 大约持平 ($1.18B → $1.19B)；股价从 4 月低点已经涨了约 3.8 倍。"seats 不会消失"这个预期修复已经发生并且定价了；要再翻一倍，需要 seats 真正加速或用量收入起来，目前两者都没有证据。

逐条结论

Thesis 环节结论证据强度一句话
E01 付费 seats 仍在净增长成立中18 场全部说在涨，零个数字；"稳定"而非加速
E02 增长来自非开发者方向成立，数字不可比弱-中非开发者占比一年里从 48% 说到 65%，主要是口径变了
E03 Coding agent 对 seats 是净利好只证明"没变坏"弱唯一数字"快 5%"有选择偏差；工作量涨得比 seat 快得多
E04 打包和提价提升 ARPU成立中-强TWC 100 万 seats、10 倍 credits、版本升级 +40%；但 TWC 升级时 seat 数不变
E05 合成可持续的 secular 加速证据不足弱迁移贡献持续到 FY29 以后，FY27 收入见底，20% CAGR 目标已撤
E06 预期预期修复已定价强FY27 有机净新增 ARR 持平；股价 $57 → $215

对照你的两条标准

  • 好公司：生意本身稳。seat 没有萎缩，Fortune 500 覆盖 85% 但只占约 10% 收入 (还有渗透空间)，Jira 每人每月 $7-8 便宜到很难被替换。问题在股东回报：SBC 和 FCF 差不多大 (我们的 primer 和 mapping 结论)，seat 增长再稳，落到股东手里的现金也不多
  • 两年一倍：现价约 $215，翻倍是 $430。primer 的 bull case 是 $352 (base $166)。要到 $430，需要 seats 从"稳定"变成"加速"，或者用量收入成规模；这次核对的 54 份材料里两者都没有证据
  • 按你的规则：现价两条都不满足，应该放弃，或者放进观察名单等价格。primer 的买点 $150-165 仍然适用

Follow-up

时间盯什么什么结果说明什么
2026-11-05 Q1 FY27云收入 vs 指引 28.5%；管理层说 seat 扩张是"稳定"还是"加速"；有没有第一次给 seat 数还是只说"strong"而不给数，说明拿不出加速的数字；净新增 ARR 跑赢"持平"的指引，才说明有机增长在加速
2026-12-03 起Rovo credit 超额收费开始 (Mizuho 估约 10% 云客户会被收费)Q2/Q3 FY27 如果管理层开始单独报 credit 收入，说明 agent 工作量开始变现；只报 MAU 说明量很小
2027-02 Q2 FY27TWC 客户数和 seats 更新 (上次是 2026 年 2 月的 1,000 家 / 100 万)TWC 增速放缓，说明打包带来的一次性提价在消化完
持续Agent 怎么收费：会不会给 agent 发 license，flex 承诺制推广到多大如果走 flex 和 credits，seat 数会越来越不是收入的口径，模型要改按用量看
可以约的专家渠道商或大客户 IT 采购：续约时 Jira/Confluence seat 同比变化；新增 seat 里非开发者占多少；有没有用 service account 接 agent这是目前所有材料里最缺的一手数据

证据限制：管理层材料占了证据的大头，而管理层每次说 seat 都不给数；专家里真正讲 Atlassian 的只有前 VP 和一家渠道商，前 VP 的核心论据来自他在 ServiceNow 的经历，渠道商的增长集中在国防单一客户；专家纪要大多只检索了相关段落，没有通读。

反常识 Check List

按对 thesis 的冲击大小排序。每条先说人话，再放原文。

1. FY27 指引隐含有机净新增 ARR 持平 → 冲击 E05/E06

23% 的 ARR 增速里约 1 个点来自收购 DX，指引 18% 意味着明年新增的 ARR 金额和今年差不多。Morgan Stanley 直接问是不是 seat 扩张在放缓，CFO 只讲审慎和宏观，没有回答。

2. CFO：seat 扩张速率"稳定 4-6 个季度" → 冲击 E01 的"加速"

这是管理层对 seat 速度最具体的一句话。稳定说明没变坏，但也说明云收入从 20% 出头加速到 31% 不是 seat 加速带来的。

3. TWC 升级后 seat 数不变，"多两位数"部分是机械对齐 → 冲击 E01/E04

CEO 在 Investor Day 说升级 TWC 后 Jira、Confluence、Loom 的 seat 数是一样的，多的是 AI credits。所谓 TWC 客户 seat 多两位数，CEO 自己说部分是打包要求几个产品 seat 数对齐。这是提价，不是更多人在用。

4. AI 工作量涨得比人快得多 → 冲击 E03

MCP 调用一个季度涨 400%，人用网页和手机的量几乎没变。这句可以理解为在说服务成本，但字面上说明 AI 带来的工作量不需要更多人登录。

5. 渠道商：agent 用一个 service account 就能操作 Jira → 冲击 E03

同一家渠道商一边说今年会卖更多 seat，一边说长期不需要每人一个 license，并预期 2-3 年后开发者大规模裁员；Service Collection 卖得好是因为 JSM 不再单卖，客户续约被强制转入。

6. Atlassian 自己：开发者效率提升约 25%，同时放缓招聘 → 冲击 E03

这正是市场担心客户会做的事，公司自己先做了。

7. CEO 承认 JSM 同客户口径下有效率化 → 冲击 E01

被问到同店口径 seat 是否萎缩，CEO 承认部分客户在用 AI 提效，被 HR、财务等新场景抵消。

8. 2025 年 10 月：云指引上调只反映迁移，有机增长指引反而下调 → 冲击 E05

9. 卖方和渠道：预算降优先级、Linear 抢份额、SMB 裁员 seat 掉 20%

这三条都是二手转述或个例，量级不能外推，但方向一致：小客户和开发者重度场景是 seat 最脆弱的地方。

Thesis 证据分析

按生意上的问题组织。每节先给判断，再列关键原文，最后可以展开该节全部原文。绿色是支持 thesis 的命题，红色是反对，灰色是口径不齐或中性。

Seats 还在涨吗

判断：在涨，没有萎缩；但没有一个数字，速度是"稳定"而不是"加速"。

2025 年 5 月到 2026 年 9 月，管理层在每一场业绩会和卖方会议上都说 seat 在扩张，而且越说越强：从 2025 年的"very consistent""absolute seat growth"，到 2026 年 Q3、Q4 两次把云收入超预期直接归因于"cross-sell 和 seat expansion"。CRO 说续约时没有看到 seat 减少。Investor Day 上 CEO 放了一张"剔除迁移的云 seats"曲线，让大家"拿尺子量"，但没给任何刻度。

能说明速度的只有 CFO 的"稳定 4-6 个季度"。外部证据也是如此：渠道商 Q1 看到 seat 大涨，但几乎全来自一个国防客户 (300 → 5,000，专家自己怀疑对方配不齐人)，非国防业务"平淡、符合预期"。

展开全部 46 条原文 (按时间)
  • 支持 FQ3 云收入超预期的两大来源之一是好于预期的 paid seat expansion（未量化）
    Yes. Thanks for the question. From a Cloud perspective, we did deliver 25% year-over-year revenue growth. That was better than we expected coming into the quarter. The core underlying performance in our Cloud business was very similar to Q2 with trends from Q2 continuing into Q3. The variance to our expectations was driven by two factors. It was better-than-expected paid seat expansion and Data Center migrations. All the other drivers in this part of our business, whether it's cross-sell of additional products or adoption of higher-value additions or top of funnel performance or customer retention, those were all in line with our expectations going into the quarter. In terms of looking at the level of beat relative to the guidance in Q3 versus first half, I would just start by saying that in both Q1 and Q2, those were exceptionally strong quarters where we executed really well against a very robust set of opportunities in pipeline. Then I think fundamentally, Q3 is simply a seasonally slower quarter for us. So there was less opportunity for the level of outperformance you saw in the first half of the year. And then I know not specific to Cloud but more generally, there were some mechanical dynamics, particularly in Data Center and Marketplace that negatively impacted revenue growth on the margin. And I think taking all of this into account helps explain most of the difference between our H1 performance to guidance in Q3. 2025-05-01 · S&P 业绩会纪要 · Joseph L. Binz (CFO, 明示) · L295
  • 支持 管理层认为有巨大的用户扩张空间（版本 + 更多知识工作者 wall-to-wall），属愿景未量化
    Sales of premium enterprise editions are up over 40% year-on-year. And the cloud customers who have deployed Rovo agents to now tens of thousands of unique workflows connected to automation are seeing great value. So I would say that from that position of strength, we feel very good about the capabilities that we've built and we want to get them into as many hands as possible and as fast as we can amongst our user base, right? We believe that by maximizing usage and adoption, we -- yes, we've always been patient with monetization, but we think we have a massive user expansion opportunity, both in terms of additions and also growing wall-to-wall with more knowledge workers in those organizations. So that fits really well with the way that we think about building software in general. Again, we're always very thoughtful about balancing growth and capital usage. As we mentioned at the Investor Day, we've done some amazing work in R&D to make those capabilities able to be deployed at a reasonable cost, which is obviously a gateway in order to be able to make this sort of a move. 2025-05-01 · S&P 业绩会纪要 · Michael Cannon-Brookes (CEO, 明示) · L126
  • 支持 上云客户的扩张（加 seats）和交叉销售都比本地部署更强
    Sure. So Atlassian historically had a server business. Think of this as a perpetual license model. So we would basically have customers buy the server software and really only pay us a maintenance fee there on. So several years ago, we decided to switch to a cloud model for two reasons. One, technologically cloud would allow us to offer innovation that server technology would not; and second, we wanted to shift to a subscription model from the perpetual license model. And we began this journey in 2020 and we are in the -- I would describe it as the final leg of that journey. And we've been phenomenally successful with that transition. So we've done better than what we had internally modeled in terms of customer churn. We've done better in terms of what we expected expansion to look like. So the fundamental thing to understand about the business is that the shift from perpetual to subscription business is complete for us in terms of we have -- we no longer have a perpetual license model, so the end of life the server product and data center and cloud both have a subscription model. And customers who adopt our cloud products, like I said before, show higher degrees of expansion that is adding more seats as well as higher degrees of cross-sell, adding more products. And we have a lot more cloud products than we do server products naturally because server was the legacy business. And over the years, we've managed to successfully migrate a lot of our server customers. So we went from the business being entirely server in DC, well over 90% of our customers now around cloud and 90% of our customers now choose cloud. And the set of customers, I would describe our journey as the leg that is about to come is that we have a set of enterprise customers that are some of our largest and most complex customers that have regulatory rules, that have constraints that need them to run hybrid environments, those are the customers that we're focused on over the next couple of years to bring them over to the cloud platform. I would describe the migration overall as having been extremely successful and better than what we expected. 2025-05-29 · S&P 卖方会议纪要 · Anutthara Bharadwaj (President) · L264
  • 支持 过去 4 个季度 seat 增长很稳定、很健康（没给数字）
    I think what's also interesting, when we look at our seat growth, our seat growth has been very consistent over the past 4 quarters. So we continue to see really healthy seat growth. What's been also really interesting is when you cut it by tech startups, right? You would imagine that tech start-ups tend to be the most tech-forward companies. Their seat expansion patterns have been consistent with the seat expansion patterns that you see with SMBs across other industries. So that's a really encouraging sign for me, which again, I think, shows you our ability to serve not just probably developers, but also other types of users. And then earlier, you mentioned our focus on enterprise, right? And so while we certainly drive a lot of our growth via seat expansion, we also have developed different growth vectors in terms of upgrading people to premium and enterprise additions upselling and focusing on these enterprises, cross-selling across the broader portfolio of products and then pricing, right, capturing it via consumption pricing or perhaps per seat pricing over time. So I think we feel really good about kind of the culmination of these different vectors and our ability to drive durable growth no matter how the world continues to evolve with Gen AI. 2025-06-03 · S&P 卖方会议纪要 · Martin Lam (Head of IR) · L263
  • 支持 seat 扩张模式没有偏离，继续看到健康的、绝对值的 seat 增长
    Yes. And again, I think it's important to remember that fundamentally Atlassian is a collaboration company, right? Like all of our products facilitate collaboration. And so as the output increases of software because no doubt it will, there become all these new problems that humans have to collaborate together on and solve and Brian echoed all of that, right? Like how do you manage all this new software? How do you sell it? How do you get feedback from what customers want so that you can iterate on that software? Again, there's an abundance of new problems that get created. I think it's also important to remember that already today, 50% of our seats are technical in nature, so that's developers, engineering, IT. And already, we serve 50% that are nontechnical. So those business users, and we'll continue to push more in that direction. When you actually look at some of these SMB tech start-ups that you would expect to be much more kind of embracing or forward tech embracing, when you look at their patterns of seat expansion, it's been consistent for the past year. So that's good. And then also when you contrast that against SMBs of similar size in different industries, it's very consistent. So you don't see any deviation in kind of seat expansion patterns. Continue to see very healthy seat growth, continue to see absolute seat growth. I think the other interesting anecdote is when we look at some of these CEOs or people that make kind of bombastic proclamations, when you look at the underlying customer data, we actually see seat expansion within a lot of these companies. And so it's -- I think we feel really good as we continue to see seat growth because of all these initiatives and projects that continue to develop, as Brian said, but also as we kind of continue to push more to serving these nontechnical users. 2025-06-04 · S&P 卖方会议纪要 · Martin Lam (Head of IR) · L228
  • 支持 Q4 云超预期的主要来源是 paid seat expansion、cross-sell 和迁移
    On Cloud revenue guidance coming into the quarter, we'd assume some negative impact on paid seat expansion rates and cloud migrations from macro uncertainty and our Enterprise go-to-market evolution. And to your point, neither one of those were fully materialized. We continue to benefit from relatively stable macro and business trends from the quarter. In terms of our Cloud performance in Q4, paid seat expansion, cross- sell and migrations were the primary drivers of the better-than-expected results with in-line performance across the other Cloud drivers. Notably, the rate of paid seat expansion in SMB and enterprise continued to demonstrate quarter-to- quarter stability. In Data Center revenue was in line with our guidance with growth driven by pricing, and that was partially offset by strong migrations to cloud. Data Center growth also benefited from the renewal of several large customer agreements in the quarter. 2025-08-07 · S&P 业绩会纪要 · Joseph L. Binz (CFO, 明示) · L278

  • 支持 上季度迁移升级的 seats 数同比翻倍（迁移口径，不是新增 seat）
    Thanks, Kash. Look, I always appreciate the applause, I guess. Let me say a few things about Ascend and cloud migrations. Firstly, the partner and customer reaction has been fantastic. I think that's because we have well telegraphed to our customers, our partners, the entire community, that cloud is the future. It's the best experience for our customers. So we had very little surprise. We're very thoughtful and long term about how we've managed the transition, I think, and the reaction has been very positive as a result. I think the removal of technical barriers and the delivery of innovation in the cloud has been a combination of effects that's really bringing that through. You see that in the number of customers that mentioned to me, for example, AI is one of the big reasons that they are moving to the cloud. We've learned an awful lot about how to help those customers manage that upgrade through the fast shift program, through our amazing partner network, through a lot of different things over the last 5-plus years. And you see that in doubling the number of migrations -- seats that were upgraded in the last quarter that doubled year- on-year. That's a huge achievement for us. And it is thanks to those partners that you talked about and everybody else, and you see it in us raising the cloud revenue outlook. So I think cloud is ready for our customers. You see that in FedRAMP Moderate in government cloud, isolated cloud, multi-cloud strategy. So we feel incredibly bullish that we have the experience to do this. We feel that it's the right experience for our customers, and we are thoughtfully managing that migration as you pointed out. So I don't think the playbook has necessarily changed as much as every year and every quarter that goes by, we get better at it. We did lots and lots of large migrations last quarter across different geographies, different industries, pretty much any country that I go to, any city, I can point to a large customer in that geography in their industry that has already moved.   • 支持 Seat 扩张跨技术和非技术用户；企业和 SMB 的扩张率稳定了 4-6 个季度（稳定，非加速；未拆分新客户/存量）
    Yes. Thanks for the question, Ittai. In terms of where we see the expansion, I'd say it's broad-based. It's across both tech and non-tech users.   • 反对 未来 1-2 年开发者 headcount 只增 0-5%，纯按人头 seat 的开发工具将承压
    核心不在于有多少开发者，而在于有多少基础设施和工作流任务需要自动化。如果对公司做一个方向性的量化，我认为未来一到两年内，开发者员工人数（headcount）的年增长率可能只有0%到5%，但软件产出和变更量可能会增长15%到30%。因此，传统的纯按人头席位（per-seat）收费的开发者工具将面临压力。但那些能够将AI Agent、自动化、工作流规模、安全治理以及基于用量（consumption-based）进行商业化变现的平台，反而有机会推动单开发者支出（spend per developer）上升。因此，真正的变化并不是AI导致开发者变少、进而引发开发工具支出下降；而是AI带来单开发者产出提高和更多机器生成的工作，促使开发工具定价逐渐从单纯的人类席位转向席位加Agent及用量收费。对于Jira、Confluence、CI/CD自动化和DevSecOps来说，这一趋势至关重要。 2026-09-23 · Funda · 专家 · L319
    来源背景：Strategy Executive，美国头部电信运营商（前任职）

  • 支持 CEO 称 Jira 与 TWC 刚经历最好的增长年，用户规模大幅加速
    * Cannon-Brookes says Jira and the Teamwork Collection just had their best year of growth, accelerating at massive scale in users. * Atlassian sells products, including via DX, showing customers token spend by model and feature and where to cut it; he says enterprises are increasingly price-sensitive as AI becomes a growing budget line. * In Amsterdam, Atlassian announced agent-to-agent integrations with Figma and Databricks, so Confluence users can query Databricks or Snowflake through the Teamwork Graph, making Atlassian a hub rather than a bypassed destination (our read). * Atlassian runs many fine-tuned open-weight models, including a lot of NVIDIA Nemotron, and his biggest change of mind this year is inference prices falling further than expected, which suggests margin upside on AI features (our read). 2026-10-12 · TMTB (Substack) · Mike Cannon-Brookes（摘要） · L384
    来源背景：Atlassian CEO Mike Cannon-Brookes（摘要转述）

谁在加 seat：非开发者

判断：方向成立，非开发者确实占一半以上；但"增长最快的是非开发者"从来没给数，占比数字前后口径不同，不能拿来算趋势。

同一个指标，一年里说法变了四次：2025 年 6 月是"Jira 用户 52% 技术、48% 非技术"，第二天变成"50% 的 seats"，2026 年 5 月变成"Jira 约 2/3、Confluence 7/10、JSM 超 3/4"，6 月又变成"平台 2/3 的活跃用户"。如果 48% → 65% 是真实变化，在 Jira 用户整体增长 10% 的假设下，开发者用户要一年少 26%，这和管理层说开发者 seat 也在涨矛盾 (见 Information Add-on)。更可能是口径从"用户"换成了"活跃用户"或换了产品范围。

Service Collection 也一样：2 月说"超过 2/3 客户用于非 IT"，4 月说"60%"。外部唯一相关的一手案例是反例：Flock Safety 打算把财务、HR、采购的服务台给 AI 原生的 Console，而不是 JSM。

Coding agent 是加 seat 还是减 seat

判断：证据只支持"agent 还没让 seat 变少"，不支持"agent 让 seat 加速"。AI 带来的是工作量，而工作量涨得比 seat 快得多。

管理层的逻辑链是：写代码的成本下降 → 软件变多、写软件的人变多 (包括财务、HR、市场) → 规划、追踪、协作需求变多 → 更多 Jira seats。支撑这条链的数字只有一个：用 AI 编码工具的数千家客户，Jira 任务多 5%、MAU 多 5%、seat 扩张快 5%。CEO 在 2026 年 2 月主动澄清这不是 5% 的扩张率，而是比别人高 5%，并承认这批都是"增长型的前沿公司"，选择偏差很明显。"Rovo 客户 ARR 增速 2 倍"同理，是相关性。

反过来看，工作量的数字比 seat 大一个量级：经 MCP 建的 Jira 工作项是 4 倍，MCP/CLI 月活超过 100 万，MCP 调用一个季度涨 400%，而人通过网页和手机用产品"几乎没变"。这说明 AI 带来的价值主要以"不用登录的工作量"出现。这部分要变现，靠的是 credits 和用量，不是 seat。管理层在这点上也没有定论：CRO 说 agent 怎么收费"是我们和同行都在讨论的问题"；CFO 在 Citi 被问 agent 数量远超人类 seat 时，只回答了 credits。

专家这边：Atlassian 前 VP 说 AI 让客户做更多原本做不到的工作，所以 seat 不降反升，但这个观察来自他在 ServiceNow 的经历，数字是他自己说的"随便举例"。反方更多：渠道商说一个 service account 就够；Funda 的电信高管说开发者人数未来 1-2 年只增 0-5%；Co-operators 首席 AI 官说长期会减工程师；monday.com 前员工说纯 per-seat 厂商"要么转用量，要么死"。

  • 支持 谁能创造软件在快速变化，非技术人员也能创造软件，这对 Atlassian 有利
    The belief around what does AI mean for our platforms because there's one school of thought which worries a lot about, oh, will AI put developers out of work and what happens to seats, et cetera. And then there's another school of thought around what is actually happening on the ground and how is work evolving and what role can companies play in that. At Atlassian, fundamentally, we think about it as AI is going to cause more software to be created. And more software being created is a good thing for us as a business because we are in the business of serving teams that build software. Now the way that teams build software is changing rapidly, who gets to create software is changing rapidly. So it's no longer the technical developer, but also the nontechnical consumer that can create software. So for Atlassian, that's a good thing in terms of how do we serve these nontechnical customers in addition to the traditional technical developer that creates software. The good thing for us is that well over half our business has been serving nontechnical users over the last couple of decades. And so for us we think about the opportunity is really in terms of how do we build these brand-new workflows that serve these nontechnical audiences, which is what we do with Rovo Studio, with Rovo Agents. And how do you really unlock knowledge from various different silos and bring it in service of these new people who are creating a lot of software. In terms of the risk that creates, there is a risk that -- given all the fraud in the market, there is a risk of fragmentation where enterprises end up trying various different tools. But we see it mostly as a cycle. So as people try out new different tools and see what's in it for them and how to use it, typically, enterprises come back to consolidating on a platform and the fact that we can serve different kinds of teams, customer support teams, developer teams, marketing teams, HR teams, we are able to provide that one consolidated platform at Atlassian.   • 反对 AI 压缩开发周期，原来多个角色交接的工作集中到通才身上
    Yes. So Jira is extremely valuable in the context of any company that's deployed it, not because it's a project management tool, but because it's a workflow tool. So Jira has access to so many different workflows along how work happens. For example, what's the customer reporting in terms of issues and what -- how is that issue getting resolved? What's the full request that went into production? What was the incident in production and how did we manage that incident? All of those workflows get captured in Jira. So in the context of AI-induced software development, what changes is a lot of those cycles of software development now are getting compressed. So earlier where you used to have one developer, one designer, one program manager, one DevOps engineer and do all these handoffs, a lot of that power is now getting diffused and concentrated with generalists, so to speak. So Jira, therefore, becomes less of a project management tracking tool in terms of, let me write down what's on the road map, but more an orchestration engine. So irrespective of what kind of software you're building, as long as it's sophisticated enough and it's used in the real world, it will involve more than one person doing the job. And how do you coordinate that work when there's more than one person? The unit of coordination may not look like a bug or a story point. The unit of coordination might look like AI agents kicking off workflows. So now how do you orchestrate these multiple agents working together? Where do they start and stop? How do you push notifications and control all of those? And Jira is the perfect point for that. It's the perfect surface area for it because it has visibility across all of your teams. So we see Jira very much going in that direction, and that's what we keep building inside of. 2025-09-10 · S&P 卖方会议纪要 · Anutthara Bharadwaj (President) · L148

    Rebuttal: History shows that when you reduce the friction of a task, demand for that task increases. As AI makes employees more productive, companies often hire more people to attack higher-order problems. More seats! Furthermore, top-tier SaaS is already pivoting to consumption/outcome-based pricing (per conversation, per resolution). This allows them to capture the value of the digital labor directly, likely expanding the TAM beyond what human "seats" could ever cap out at. 6. Legacy SaaS will struggle to transition to outcomes Rebuttal: They are the only ones capable of selling outcomes. To sell an "outcome," you must be able to measure the "before" and "after." The System of Record holds the historical truth. ServiceNow knows exactly how long an IT ticket took to resolve in 2023 vs 2026. They are in the absolute best position to prove value and charge for the outcome because they own the ledger that defines what an "outcome" is. A startup has no baseline data to prove they delivered the result. 2026-01-29 · VIC · VIC 作者引用的 X 帖（Marcelo Lima） · L76

When I was there, any time we have a renewal, it was very easy to negotiate a renewal because you can literally just say, "Hey, you're already using our CI/CD, you can move off of GitLab, but it's going to take you two years to do it. It makes no sense." The cost of switching was so high, you can upcharge them 50% more on renewal and they wouldn't even want to move. They wouldn't even consider it to just take the 50% increase. Now, because of AI, it's actually pretty easy to migrate off of GitLab or everything is just easy to migrate in general. You really have to be prepared to win that customer and renew that customer without the threat of like, "Oh, it's just too sticky," because nothing is really sticky anymore. It's easy for anyone to just move off of anything these days. 2026-06-01 · Third Bridge · Steve Xu · L98

  • 口径不齐 AI 没有让研发人数缩减，但工程团队基本持平、低个位数增长，产出增长快得多
    从我个人了解到的情况来看，实际情况介于两者之间。AI 提效并没有导致研发人数（developer headcount）同比例缩减；更贴切的描述是工程团队人数保持相对平稳（relatively flat），处于低个位数增长（low single-digit growth），而产出增长得快得多。需要明确的是，生产力提升（productivity gain）并不会 1:1 转化为端到端交付提升（end-to-end delivery gain）。但对公司而言，AI 编程工具确实让单个开发者产出了更多代码、PR 和变更。很多代码现在都是 AI 写的，占比可达 60%、70%、80% 甚至 90%，但这反而极大地增加了下游工作量。公司现在面临更多的 code review、CI/CD 运行、安全扫描（security scanning）、测试、文档、工单（tickets）、部署以及可观测性需求。到了2026年，我认为大约 80% 到 90% 的代码都由 AI 生成，但同时也带来了许多工作流问题。 对于像 Atlassian 这样的厂商（Jira 和 Confluence），不能简单地将其收入按开发者席位（developer seat）来建模。实际上，Jira 和 Confluence 本身正在从单纯的人类开发者席位工具，转变为融合人类与 AI Agent 工作流的记录系统（system of record）。Jira 正在明显向管理人类与 AI Agent 的规划、治理和度量层（planning, governance, and measurement layer）推进。 而像 Ansible 这类工具则有所不同。AI 产生了更多软件工程基础设施变更后，公司反而需要更多的自动化（automation）、配置管理（configuration management）、策略执行（policy enforcement）和可重复部署（repeatable deployment）。 2026-09-23 · Funda · 专家 · L313
    来源背景：Strategy Executive，美国头部电信运营商（前任职）
  • 支持 AI 编码带来更多 review/测试/工单等下游工作，Atlassian 捕获工作流规模增长而非 seat 增长，支出价值不会下降
    我看到的比较明显的趋势可以分为两类：一类是实现AI编码（AI coding）商业化变现的工具；另一类则是享受到AI带来的下游工作流拉动效应（downstream workflow pull-through）的工具。 第一类最直接的代表是GitHub Copilot、Anthropic的Claude Code，以及Cursor这类AI原生编码工具。它们直接拉高了单开发者支出，因此即使开发者人数不增长，AI许可证和用量本身仍能持续增长。 第二类包括Atlassian、GitLab、Harness以及Red Hat的Ansible这类工作流和DevOps平台。因为AI编码生成了更多的代码和PR，后续必然会带来更多的代码审查（review）、测试、CI/CD、部署、安全扫描、工单处理和自动化需求。因此它们捕获的并非单纯的席位增长，而是工作流规模（workflow volume）的增长。 举个例子，Atlassian就是一个典型代表。即使工程人员编制基本持平，如果每位开发者管理的工程项目、工单、AI生成的变更以及Agent工作流都在增加，那么公司对Jira和Confluence这类记录系统（system of record）及工作流平台的支出价值就不会下降。Atlassian自身也在将Jira拓展为面向人类与Agent的统一工作管理层。 如果总结我目前最明确的判断：AI原生编码工具无疑是第一波受益者；而工作流、CI/CD自动化和DevSecOps则是第二波拉动效应的受益者。真正面临压力的是那些纯按人类席位收费、功能单一、且无法对Agent、自动化或用量进行商业化变现的开发者单点工具（point tools）。因为未来开发者席位增长可能会非常缓慢，但机器生成的工作负载增长会非常迅速。 2026-09-23 · Funda · 专家 · L331
    来源背景：Strategy Executive，美国头部电信运营商（前任职）

  • 支持 CEO 认为 agent 把软件变成数据库的说法只适用于平庸功能任务，不适用协作重的企业工作流
    Cannon-Brookes rejects host Harry Stebbings’ framing that agents turn software into mere databases, saying that holds only for “pedestrian” functional tasks (like buying a charging cable) but not for collaboration-heavy enterprise workflows. He says Atlassian operates one of the top three-to-five MCP servers globally, enabling agents to read and write the Teamwork Graph, but argues that if Atlassian’s interfaces stop evolving to solve customer problems, the company loses relevance to that experience. He envisions a mixed future of horizontal agents, vertical deep-task agents, and persistent interfaces coexisting — not one “Uber agent” or single magic button. As evidence interfaces still carry commercial value, he cites Confluence generating north of $1.5B ARR, growing over 25% annually at scale — large enough to be a standalone public company — despite writing text seeming commoditizable. 2026-10-12 · TMTB (Substack) · Mike Cannon-Brookes（摘要） · L391
    来源背景：Atlassian CEO Mike Cannon-Brookes（摘要转述）

打包和提价：ARPU 提升

判断：成立，而且是过去一年加速里最实在的部分；但它本质上是提价和交叉销售，不是更多人在用。

Teamwork Collection (Jira + Confluence + Loom + Rovo 打包，credits 是单品的 10 倍) 不到三个季度卖出 1,000 家、100 万 seats，管理层说客户升级的头号原因是 10 倍 AI credits。打包要求各产品 seat 数一致，只买了 Jira 的客户等于被带着补齐 Confluence 和 Loom。IR 说升级 TWC 的客户用户数多约 10%。"TWC 客户每实例付费 seats 是单品客户的 4-5 倍"是横截面对比，更可能说明大客户更爱买 TWC。

另外两条 ARPU 杠杆：Premium/Enterprise 版本销售同比约 +40%；云每年提价，Mizuho 测算最新一轮挂牌价 +7-10%。Jira 每人每月约 $7-8 (管理层两次说法不一)，CFO 认为还有很大提价空间。迁移本身也是提价：小客户持平到 +5%，大客户最高 2 倍，第一年大约 1:1，第二、三年逐步恢复全价。

需要注意的是 Service Collection：渠道商说它是唯一有起色的 collection，因为 JSM 不再单卖，续约被强制转入。

  • 反对 用户数下降时无法靠提价 50% 维持 per-user 收入，客户在谈判中占上风
    **Expert:** I honestly cannot think of a world where it wouldn't work. I think that you can prove it with… I think the consumption-based model is the only one that's going to work for any company that's in SaaS at this point, in my opinion, because naturally, you're going to have less developers doing things. You need less people to do things now with the tools at hand. Most of the budget right now is going towards the large LLM models, Anthropic and OpenAI. Really, when you move all that money of a company to those purchases, then you have less money to spend on seats on GitLab or any other tools. Company is naturally going to figure out ways to cut down the number of users or if you could pivot to consumption model, you can actually keep those contracts steady and you don't have to churn in the next year or two at renewal. The consumption model is basically a way to financially… Accounting-wise, you are financially redoing the contract so that the customer doesn't feel like they're not getting the value add. Because if they lose users and you're still on user pricing, you can't convince them to pay 50% more per user. That's just not going to work. The cost of switching now is so much easier than before. The customer is always going to win that negotiation. If you move to consumption, it's like you can find a different way of modelling the next contract at renewal of like, "Hey, yes, you have less users, but here's how much more you're using. This is why this new pricing model works for you." It's easier to justify to a CFO who's trying to sign the contract. **LF:** Would you expect GitLab to price by tokens, tasks or some other measure within the new pricing model? **Expert:** It's going to be tokens. I think they haven't released what their strategy is, but it sounds like it's going to be tokens and very much like Claude and Cursor, all those other companies. 2026-06-01 · Third Bridge · Steve Xu · L45

是 secular 加速，还是迁移和提价

判断：管理层把 Q3、Q4 的超预期归给 seat 和交叉销售，这本身可信；但整体增速里有一块会持续好几年、最终会结束的迁移红利，而有机部分的指引并没有加速。

管理层的说法：迁移"符合预期"，不是超预期的来源，超预期来自 cross-sell 和 seat expansion。CFO 在 Citi 也说大部分增量不靠挂牌价，而靠加 seat、升版本、从单品升到 collection。NRR 120% 以上，连续几个季度往上走。

但同一批材料也显示：迁移每年给云收入贡献中到高个位数的增长 (FY25、FY26 都是)，DC 2029 年 3 月停服，管理层预计迁移在 FY28-29 加速；迁移后的大客户 3 年 ARR 涨约 1.75 倍，说明迁移红利会拖很长的尾巴。Q3 FY26 的 DC 收入多出约 $50M，是 3 月提价前客户提前采购。2025 年 10 月云指引上调完全来自迁移，剔除 DC 停售后有机增长指引反而下调 0.5 个点。Investor Day 上管理层撤掉了 3 年 20% 收入 CAGR 的目标，说 FY27 收入增速见底。

所以"seats + ARPU = secular 加速"在数字上还看不到：能看到的是 seat 稳定增长 + 迁移 + 打包提价的叠加，叠加里只有 seat 那一块是长期的。

    Sure. So Atlassian historically had a server business. Think of this as a perpetual license model.   • 口径不齐 变现重点依次是上云迁移、TWC、版本升级（偏一次性的迁移和打包杠杆）
    What we've done maybe a little bit different than everybody else is we've launched Teamwork Collection at our team events in -- earlier in April this year. So we took Jira, Confluence and Loom and consolidated that into one bundle called Teamwork Collection and then included Rovo in that and gave our customers 10x the Rovo or AI credits that they would have received if they just bought the product stand-alone.   • 支持 Confluence ARR 超 $1.5B，年增 25%+
    Cannon-Brookes rejects host Harry Stebbings’ framing that agents turn software into mere databases, saying that holds only for “pedestrian” functional tasks (like buying a charging cable) but not for collaboration-heavy enterprise workflows. He says Atlassian operates one of the top three-to-five MCP servers globally, enabling agents to read and write the Teamwork Graph, but argues that if Atlassian’s interfaces stop evolving to solve customer problems, the company loses relevance to that experience. He envisions a mixed future of horizontal agents, vertical deep-task agents, and persistent interfaces coexisting — not one “Uber agent” or single magic button. As evidence interfaces still carry commercial value, he cites Confluence generating north of $1.5B ARR, growing over 25% annually at scale — large enough to be a standalone public company — despite writing text seeming commoditizable. 2026-10-12 · TMTB (Substack) · Mike Cannon-Brookes（摘要） · L397
    来源背景：Atlassian CEO Mike Cannon-Brookes（摘要转述）

市场预期和股价

判断：市场的担忧从"seat 会消失"切换成了"有机增长有没有在加速"。前一个问题管理层答得很充分，后一个问题 FY27 指引的回答是没有。

卖方在业绩会上反复问 seat：2026 年 2 月 Morgan Stanley 说"市场担心 seat 模型"，4 月 Mizuho 说"市场非常担心 seat 压缩"，管理层每次都用定性说法回应，"如果说有什么，我们看到的是相反的"。8 月 FY27 指引出来后，问题变成了"18% 是不是 seat 在放缓"，Wolfe 算出隐含有机净新增 ARR 持平。卖方观点分化：RBC 认为能持续 20% 以上，空头认为"审慎"可能在掩盖真实放缓。

股价已经把第一个问题的答案反映进去了：周收盘价从 2025 年 2 月的 $314 跌到 2026 年 4 月的 $57 (-82%)，现在 $215，是低点的 3.8 倍。最大的两次跳涨就是 Q3 业绩 + Investor Day 和 Q4 业绩，也就是管理层集中反驳"seats 会消失"的两个时点。

数据：IBKR 周 K 线收盘价，2024-10-21 至 2026-10-19。你提到的"跌 78%、涨 2 倍以上"，按周收盘是跌 82%、涨 3.8 倍。

    The entire ~$49M Cloud beat came from core Jira/Confluence seat expansion and Collections cross-sell; Data Center migrations were in line and did not contribute to the beat. Cloud has now accelerated from 26% to 29% to 31% over the past three quarters, materially weakening the argument that recent upside was just migration, pricing or acquisition noise. * **Guide** The F1Q and FY27 Cloud guides were substantially better than feared, while the headline +13% total revenue guide is depressed by the expected 17% Data Center decline. The 18% Subscription ARR guide nevertheless implies roughly flat organic net-new ARR dollars in FY27, so this becomes the key metric for determining whether mgmt’s second-half prudence is simply conservatism or reflects genuine decel. Mgmt cited early-year visibility, tougher second-half comparisons, the one-point FY26 DX benefit and macro uncertainty * **Enterprise** TEAM signed its largest enterprise deal ever and posted record activity across the $1M+, $3M+ and $5M+ deal cohorts. Customers above $3M of ARR grew more than 50% y/y and those above $5M grew more than 70%, while RPO accelerated to 44%, cRPO to 27% and NRR remained above 120%. Mgmt characterized this as the result of several years of product, compliance, sales and customer-success investment rather than a one-quarter unlock. 2026-08-07 · TMTB (Substack) · TMTB 作者 · L217

Agent 怎么收费：seat 之后

判断：管理层自己在为 seat 之后做准备。最大部分的价值今天仍然按 seat 收，但 credits、用量和 flex 承诺制都已经上线，agent 不按 seat 收。

CEO 说大多数应用按 seat 定价，"这是多数客户想要的买法"；同时在和大客户谈 flex：承诺一个金额，可以在 seats、应用和 collection 之间挪用。CFO 说 AI 变现今天走 TWC 的 ARPU，"以后可能更多转向用量，和 seats 并行"。IR 说用量定价会是"更大的一部分故事"，今年开始执行 credit 上限。对 thesis 的含义是：就算 agent 带来了巨大的工作量，体现在报表上也更可能是 credits 和用量，而不是 seat 数。

    The belief around what does AI mean for our platforms because there's one school of thought which worries a lot about, oh, will AI put developers out of work and what happens to seats, et cetera. And then there's another school of thought around what is actually happening on the ground and how is work evolving and what role can companies play in that. At Atlassian, fundamentally, we think about it as AI is going to cause more software to be created. And more software being created is a good thing for us as a business because we are in the business of serving teams that build software. Now the way that teams build software is changing rapidly, who gets to create software is changing rapidly. So it's no longer the technical developer, but also the nontechnical consumer that can create software. So for Atlassian, that's a good thing in terms of how do we serve these nontechnical customers in addition to the traditional technical developer that creates software. The good thing for us is that well over half our business has been serving nontechnical users over the last couple of decades. And so for us we think about the opportunity is really in terms of how do we build these brand-new workflows that serve these nontechnical audiences, which is what we do with Rovo Studio, with Rovo Agents. And how do you really unlock knowledge from various different silos and bring it in service of these new people who are creating a lot of software. In terms of the risk that creates, there is a risk that -- given all the fraud in the market, there is a risk of fragmentation where enterprises end up trying various different tools. But we see it mostly as a cycle. So as people try out new different tools and see what's in it for them and how to use it, typically, enterprises come back to consolidating on a platform and the fact that we can serve different kinds of teams, customer support teams, developer teams, marketing teams, HR teams, we are able to provide that one consolidated platform at Atlassian.   • 中性 Jira Align 定价在 Atlassian 企业协议层面打包，但仍间接随 seat 数扩展
    EXPERT: In enterprise PPM tools, pricing is almost never truly transparent, and it's typically structured as a subscription-based enterprise license, but with different metering models depending on the platform. In our evaluation, Planview and Jira Align were both priced as enterprise subscriptions, not simple per-user SaaS, typically negotiated as an annual contract based on a combination of total users, modules enabled, and overall portfolio scale, effectively making them platform licensing deals rather than strict per-seat pricing. Jira Align still indirectly scales with seat counts, like portfolio users, contributors, and read-only users. But pricing is bundled at the enterprise level with Atlassian agreements. Planview followed a similar enterprise subscription model but was even more heavily tied to modules like financial planning, capacity planning, etc., and organizational size, making it the most rigid and highest cost structure. Smartsheet, in contrast, was more clearly per user, like per member, per seat subscription model with additional paid add-ons for advanced portfolio capabilities like Control Center and DataMesh, making it more modular and scalable in cost, but less predictable at enterprise scale. 2026-04-29 · GuidePoint · Mahak Varshney · L335

  • 口径不齐 Jira/Confluence 不应按开发者 seat 建模，正转向管理人类与 agent 的记录系统
    从我个人了解到的情况来看，实际情况介于两者之间。AI 提效并没有导致研发人数（developer headcount）同比例缩减；更贴切的描述是工程团队人数保持相对平稳（relatively flat），处于低个位数增长（low single-digit growth），而产出增长得快得多。需要明确的是，生产力提升（productivity gain）并不会 1:1 转化为端到端交付提升（end-to-end delivery gain）。但对公司而言，AI 编程工具确实让单个开发者产出了更多代码、PR 和变更。很多代码现在都是 AI 写的，占比可达 60%、70%、80% 甚至 90%，但这反而极大地增加了下游工作量。公司现在面临更多的 code review、CI/CD 运行、安全扫描（security scanning）、测试、文档、工单（tickets）、部署以及可观测性需求。到了2026年，我认为大约 80% 到 90% 的代码都由 AI 生成，但同时也带来了许多工作流问题。 对于像 Atlassian 这样的厂商（Jira 和 Confluence），不能简单地将其收入按开发者席位（developer seat）来建模。实际上，Jira 和 Confluence 本身正在从单纯的人类开发者席位工具，转变为融合人类与 AI Agent 工作流的记录系统（system of record）。Jira 正在明显向管理人类与 AI Agent 的规划、治理和度量层（planning, governance, and measurement layer）推进。 而像 Ansible 这类工具则有所不同。AI 产生了更多软件工程基础设施变更后，公司反而需要更多的自动化（automation）、配置管理（configuration management）、策略执行（policy enforcement）和可重复部署（repeatable deployment）。 2026-09-23 · Funda · 专家 · L313
    来源背景：Strategy Executive，美国头部电信运营商（前任职）
  • 中性 开发工具定价从人类 seat 转向 seat + agent + 用量
    核心不在于有多少开发者，而在于有多少基础设施和工作流任务需要自动化。如果对公司做一个方向性的量化，我认为未来一到两年内，开发者员工人数（headcount）的年增长率可能只有0%到5%，但软件产出和变更量可能会增长15%到30%。因此，传统的纯按人头席位（per-seat）收费的开发者工具将面临压力。但那些能够将AI Agent、自动化、工作流规模、安全治理以及基于用量（consumption-based）进行商业化变现的平台，反而有机会推动单开发者支出（spend per developer）上升。因此，真正的变化并不是AI导致开发者变少、进而引发开发工具支出下降；而是AI带来单开发者产出提高和更多机器生成的工作，促使开发工具定价逐渐从单纯的人类席位转向席位加Agent及用量收费。对于Jira、Confluence、CI/CD自动化和DevSecOps来说，这一趋势至关重要。 2026-09-23 · Funda · 专家 · L319
    来源背景：Strategy Executive，美国头部电信运营商（前任职）
  • 反对 企业将通过统一 agent 层精简独立 workflow、ticketing、knowledge search 工具，外围 SaaS 有 10-20% 精简空间（未点名 Atlassian）
    我觉得未来确实会考虑，但公司的方向并不是自己把这种 system of record 重新做一遍，而是保留 SOR，对上层一些重复的 application 和 workflow spend 进行 consolidation。像 Salesforce、ServiceNow 已经掌握了 authoritative data、permission、workflow 和 API，公司完全可以在它们之上建立自己的 agent abstraction layer。用户未来不一定需要进入五六个不同的 SaaS UI，而是可以通过统一的 enterprise agent 发起请求，再由 agent 调用 Salesforce、ServiceNow 或其他 SOR 来完成 transaction。这样公司就可以逐步减少一些 standalone workflow、ticketing、knowledge search、basic automation 以及 thin application layer tools。 如果量化的话，我认为未来两到三年（maybe two to three years），这类外围 SaaS 即 point solution spend 大概有 10% 到 20% 的 rationalization opportunity，但并不一定会全部砍掉。省下来的资金也不一定会直接转化为 cost savings，其中一部分可能会重新投入到 SOR 的 AI module、agent platform、integration API、data layer 以及 security governance 中。因此最终可能会出现一个很有意思的现象：application 的 vendor count 下降，但 Salesforce、ServiceNow 等核心 SOR 的 wallet share 反而上升。对公司而言，目标并非单纯少花钱，而是从众多 human-facing applications 转向更少的 system of record 加上一个 common agent interaction layer。 2026-09-23 · Funda · 专家 · L391
    来源背景：Strategy Executive，美国头部电信运营商（前任职）

Thesis 之外的重要信息

  • $1M 以上客户超过 600 家，过去一年 +39%；$3M+ 客户 164 家 (+50%)，$5M+ 69 家 (+70%)。增长集中在大客户，但前 VP 说 $1M+ 客户翻倍主要是老客户合并分散的合同，不是新类型的客户
  • Fortune 500 覆盖 85%，只占约 10% 的收入。企业端渗透是管理层讲得最多的长期空间，也是 seat 增长最可能的来源
  • Rovo credits 用得越多，毛利率压力越大 (CFO，Citi L254)。用量变现起来之后，利润率不一定跟着涨
  • 竞品：Asana、monday 前员工都说在开发团队里很难撬动 Jira；Notion 在知识库评估中排在 Confluence 前面，有一家 200 人的客户完全停用了 Jira

来源检查范围身份与视角影响判断的问题采用边界
6 场业绩会 (S&P)全文CEO、CFO (Binz → Chuong)、IR只给定性，关键问题 (seat 是否放缓) 被绕开可作主要证据 (只限"管理层怎么说")

Citi 会议 (FactSet 近实时转录)全文CFO转录质量差："See growth""suite growth"；$7 那句断句缺失需交叉验证
Third Bridge：Atlassian 前 VP全文前 VP，近期在 ServiceNow核心论据来自 ServiceNow；数字自称举例；部分数字是访谈人转述公开材料需交叉验证
GuidePoint：金牌渠道商全文渠道商 CEO，主做国防增长集中在单一国防客户；长期观点是推测可作一手线索 (样本窄)
Funda、SmartBear、GitLab、Tegus全文或关键段落客户高管、同类公司前员工不是 Atlassian 数据，是同类模式的类比仅作线索
微软专家电话仅检索关键词段落微软战略客户 CTORovo 数字是转述公司口径仅作线索 (未全文检查)
竞品专家 (Asana、monday、Notion、Smartsheet 等)仅检索关键词段落竞品前员工或客户站在竞品立场仅作线索 (未全文检查)
TMTB 卖方摘要只看 TEAM 相关段落Substack 汇总卖方和管理层二手转述；个别结论 (如"$49M 超预期全部来自 seat") 比管理层原话更强需交叉验证

影响判断的问题

原话问题类型对结论的影响
"expand their Jira seats 5% faster" (2026-02-05 L85)口径未明 + 选择偏差E03 唯一的数字证据打折
"65% of Jira users are knowledge workers" vs "48% are nontechnical"口径变化E02 不能算趋势
CRO "20% expansion" vs IR "by double or by more than 10 percentage points"篇间矛盾TWC 扩张幅度取低值
"roughly $7 per……" vs "$8 per user per month"篇间矛盾 + 转录断句价格按 $7-8 区间用
"The entire ~$49M Cloud beat came from core Jira/Confluence seat expansion" (TMTB)推断过度管理层原话是"两大驱动"，没说全部
"MCP calls were up 400%... barely a blip in people actually using" (2026-08-06 L242)字面歧义可能在说服务成本，冲击 E03 的力度要打折
"half nonbusiness" (Investor Day L154)可能转录问题应为 non-technology，不影响结论

A 2026-03-05 · S&P 卖方会议纪要 · CRO Duffy：续约时没看到 seat 减少，用 coding 工具的客户 seat 多 5%，TWC 客户扩张 20%，agent 怎么变现还在讨论 14 条

文件：/tmp/claude-0/seats/mgmt/2026-03-05_Company_Conference_Presentation.txt

发言人：Brian Duffy，CRO（明示）；分析师 Keith Weiss，Morgan Stanley

阅读范围：全文 L1-L429　可信度：可作主要证据

在软件股大跌的背景下，Weiss 直接问 seat 模型和 Jevons 悖论。Duffy 说客户对话里和续约时都没看到 seat 减少，用 coding 工具的客户 seat 多 5%，TWC 客户扩张 20%（举例：一家半导体公司 4 万 seats 上线 10 天后再加 8,000）。业务端是增长最快的部分。agent 怎么变现还没定，“我们和其他公司都在讨论”。

问题：“5% increase in seats”没说基准（相对不用工具的客户？哪个时间段？）；L171 'a 5% increase in fees' 疑为转录错误（应为 seats）；“over 1 million seats in the cloud”放在 TWC 语境里，按 3/10 Lam 的说法应是 TWC 售出 100 万 seats，不是云端总 seats；TWC 扩张“20%”和 3/10 Lam 说的“多 10 个百分点以上”口径不同；销售人员：2025-06 说“目前约 400 人”，这里说“今年底约 400 人，较去年翻倍”，前后矛盾；“业务端增长最快”仍然没有量化

A 2026-03-10 · S&P 卖方会议纪要 · IR Lam：用 Cursor/Claude Code 的客户 seat 扩张快 5%，AI 50 中 60% 是客户，TWC 客户 seat 多扩张 10pp+，DC 迁移提价 0-2 倍 16 条

文件：/tmp/claude-0/seats/mgmt/2026-03-10_Company_Conference_Presentation.txt

发言人：Martin Lam，Head of IR（明示）；分析师 Thomas Blakey，Cantor Fitzgerald；另有现场 Unknown Analyst

阅读范围：全文 L1-L398　可信度：可作主要证据

这是 seat 量化最多的一篇。通过 MCP 用 Cursor/Claude Code 的数千家客户：Jira 任务多 5%、MAU 多 5%、seat 扩张快 5%。Forbes AI 50 中 60% 是客户，seat 增速远高于公司整体。企业客户在扩大 seat 数、签更长的合约；TWC 有 1,000 家客户、100 万 seats，seat 扩张比单买 Jira 多 10pp 以上。DC 迁移提价从持平/5% 到 2 倍，第一年约 1:1，第 2-3 年逐步回到全价。同时披露公司内部开发者效率提升约 25%，招聘在放缓。

问题：L188-190 的提问是 L37-39 的重复，疑似转录错误，对应回答实际是 DC 提价；“expand their seats 5% faster”（相对增速）和 3/5 Duffy 说的“5% increase in seats”口径不一致；TWC“by double or by more than 10 percentage points”表述含糊，和 Duffy 的“20% expansion”口径不同；用 coding 工具的客户可能本身就是增长更快的科技公司，有选择偏差，管理层没有控制这一点；AI 50 的“much faster”没有给数字；L326 'Does the seat growth even still that nobody wants to talk about' 转录有缺损

A 2026-04-17 · GuidePoint · Atlassian 金牌渠道商：PubSec seat 暴增、非 PubSec 符合预期；但长期看 AI 让多 seat 无必要，per-seat 将转向用量 14 条

文件：Knowledge Base/GuidePoint/2026-04/2026-04-17-atlassian-github-and-gitlab-april-2026-gold-partner-channel-check.md

发言人：Shawn Doyle，ReleaseTEAM CEO（自 1999 年），Atlassian Gold Partner，客户多为 Fortune 1000，重点 PubSec/国防（L40, L160-163 明示）

阅读范围：全文 L1-L488　可信度：可作主要证据

渠道一手数据显示 2026 Q1 seat 增长强劲，但主要来自 PubSec/国防（单客户 300→5,000 seat 申请，专家自己怀疑能否配齐人），非 PubSec 季度‘平淡、符合预期’；当下 AI 让客户增加资深开发者以审核 AI 代码，故‘会卖更多 seat’。但同一专家明确认为 AI 编码工具通过 MCP/服务账号接入 Jira 后‘不需要多 seat’，2-3 年内 Atlassian 必须转向用量计费，且到 AI 采用‘钟形曲线’顶点后会出现开发者大裁员；Service Collection 增长主要因 JSM 被强制打包续约。

问题：seat 增长主体是 PubSec/国防，单一事件驱动，不代表 Atlassian 整体；‘30-35% 增长’是该渠道商自身 Atlassian 业务，不是 Atlassian 公司增速；专家对当下（卖更多 seat）与 2-3 年后（不需要多 seat、裁员）的判断方向相反，需按时间维度区分；渠道商利益：用量计费会削弱渠道利润，其关注点带有立场

B 2026-04-26 · Tegus · Flock Safety IT 工程师：用 AI 原生 ITSM Console 叠在 JSM 之上，少招 4 名支持人员，计划用 Console 替代部分团队的 JSM 6 条

问题：对象为 GitLab，仅作同类对照；GitLab 客户集中于开发者，Atlassian 有更多非开发者 seat；专家 2025 年离职，现职 dbt，部分观察来自非 GitLab 场景；专家持有 GitLab 股票（L30 自述），立场偏负面但并非看空动机；同一渠道商（GuidePoint 文）称 GitLab 非 PubSec 未见 seat 减少，与本文冲突

问题：近实时转录质量差：'See growth'/'suite growth'/'C-PACE growth' 应为 seat growth；'Non-purpose' 应为非开发者；'Detaxe' 应为 DX；$7 per seat 所在句 L170-171 断句，缺少单位（per user per month?）和完整原话，且与 6/10 IR 说的 $8 不一致；L154-155 'north of 120% year over year growth ... Cloud MRR' 可能是 cloud NRR >120% 的转录错误；转录未标发言人，问答边界需推断
